# What was tried, and what wasn't

Generated by `scripts/attempts_ledger.py` from `data/attempts.json`; edit the ledger, not this page. How this project tuned each function: what was tried, what happened, what was kept or rejected, and what has not been tried. Every entry cites the document it comes from; numbers are quoted as the sources state them. Dates are the journal's own where it gives one; where the text gives none (entries 1-21, the development sweeps), the date is the day the commit that recorded the work was made.

Read this before tuning anything: if a variant is listed as rejected, the number that rejected it is here; if an idea is under *Not tried yet*, nobody has measured it, whatever its absence from the code suggests. Completed ideas are retained separately with their evidence.

**Verdicts**

- **kept**: adopted, and still how the project works.
- **rejected**: tried and measured, then not adopted.
- **superseded**: adopted at the time, later replaced (the replacement is usually the next row).
- **inconclusive**: measured, but no decision rests on it: a measurement round, a diagnostic, or a difference within noise.

**Idea status** marks each idea as *rejected-untested* (rejected on reasoning, never run); *not-done* (named in the docs as a next step or a gap, not done); *open-question* (a question the sources leave open); *done* (completed later; retained with its evidence). An idea rejected after it was measured is an attempt, not a not-tried item.

**Single runs are flagged.** A *runs* cell that starts with 1 is one run (per tool, per holdout or per sweep step). Where a model call is involved, that number carries run-to-run noise: five runs of the declared method moved with a standard deviation of 1.4-1.9 review-load points, more than the ~1 point assumed earlier. Read a small single-run difference as a tie. Deterministic code (scorer controls, generators, checks) repeats exactly, but a single run still shows one input, not a sample.

**Not tried yet**

- N001 *not-done*: Ensemble: combine two models; agreements auto-accept, disagreements go to review. Planned for round 2 and reported as not done, not dropped silently. ([GOAL.md: Addendum](../docs/GOAL.md#addendum-before-any-holdout-run-the-declared-methods-and-decision-rule))
- N002 *rejected-untested*: Tune prompts to guess the annotators' habits on the catch-all `general` class (F1 0.11-0.45). It would raise the score and do nothing for a digital twin. ([journal 8](../docs/JOURNAL.md#8-define-done-well-first-then-guard-the-definition-from-the-people-chasing-it-us), [EVALUATION.md: 2. The anti-Goodhart rules](../docs/EVALUATION.md#2-the-anti-goodhart-rules))
- N003 *not-done*: A prompt line about equipment nozzles. A new method that would need new, unseen drawings. ([GOAL.md: Round 5 results](../docs/GOAL.md#round-5-results-scored-after-the-pre-registration-above), [journal 46](../docs/JOURNAL.md#46-more-drawings-with-tag-text-five-runs-per-model-and-a-kit-for-the-first-timed-review))
- N004 *not-done*: Fine-tune an open-weight model on a site's own annotated sheets and re-measure. Out of scope here; the docs describe it as how the gap usually narrows, to be measured with the same suite. - N005 *not-done*: Self-hosted open-model speed on a target GPU. Only a hosted endpoint was used. ([COSTS.md: 5. What is not measured, and why](../docs/COSTS.md#5-what-is-not-measured-and-why))
- N006 *not-done*: Gemini in rounds 4 and 5 and the TE round trips. Left out by the project owner's direction to keep metered spend flat; its earlier results stay as earlier runs. ([GOAL.md: Round 5](../docs/GOAL.md#round-5-written-before-any-round-5-model-run-tag-text-on-new-public-drawings-and-replicate-runs), [MODELS.md](../docs/MODELS.md))
- N007 *not-done*: More drawings for model comparisons. The sample of drawings, not of runs, is still the limit on every comparison; no further public sets with usable keys were found. ([journal 46](../docs/JOURNAL.md#46-more-drawings-with-tag-text-five-runs-per-model-and-a-kit-for-the-first-timed-review))
- N008 *not-done*: Prospective validation of a changed method on PID2Graph Synthetic. Sheets 2 and 3 are consumed; it needs new data. ([journal 34](../docs/JOURNAL.md#34-prospective-frozen-synthetic-23-transfer-check))

| A170 | 2026-10-29 | line_layers.py pipe layer, two rules under the A162 split (trace_connections.py untouched, so the network and pairwise layers are the same by construction). (1) A 4-arm crossing counts as marked connected only if the residual ink also fills all four corners between its arms (at least the thinner run's width in pixels per corner, within the dot radius): a dot is a filled disc wider than the lines; stray pixels at a crossing (anti-aliasing, a thin line's ragged edge) touch both runs but fill only some corners, and strokes that touch only the two runs at an unmarked crossing are its own ink and join nothing. (2) A slanted residual stroke that carries on straight across a run (ink on both sides where it touches, each side reaching at least an arm's length, ARM_FRAC of a symbol side, from the run, the two sides on one straight line) does not join that run in the pipe layer: a label's leader drawn across a pipe to the next line, a dashed outline or hatching crossing a pipe. The traced output lists those points as slanted_crossings. Error classes found on development: OPEN100 sheet 1 (leaders across pipes, 5 crossing points), DEXPI C01 (26 'marked' crossings, 3 of them false junctions on the pipe; no development sheet had a real dot); the stray-pixel class was first seen on holdout sheet 6's images (allowed by A162 for naming classes), then confirmed on C01. Of the development errors left, OPEN100 sheet 5's (33 of 33 missed segments) come from line networks the tracer itself got wrong, and most others are key-graph omissions (an instrument leader or an arrow-tipped line drawn onto a pipe but not joined to it in the key), neither of which this layer can fix *Hypothesis:* False junctions from crossing-corner specks and slanted leaders are general drawing artefacts, so removing them lifts the pipe layer on drawings it was not chosen on | Development, before -> after: OPEN100 sheets 0-5 pipe-segment F1 0.911 -> 0.914 (P 0.937 -> 0.943, R 0.887; sheet 1 0.883 -> 0.907, others unchanged; junctions 154/165 found, 163 -> 161 predicted; instrument connections 0.835 unchanged); DEXPI C01 0.724 -> 0.902, C02 0.714 unchanged (DEXPI set with C03 0.681 -> 0.782; C03 0.545 unchanged; 15 -> 11 junctions predicted, 5 of 5 tees found); TE revision D 1.000 and Bayer 1.000 unchanged (instrument connections 1.000). Pairwise links identical on every sheet (OPEN100 0-5: 346, 72, 160, 92, 117, 278). Committed examples regenerated with their documented commands: sheet0_graph.json and dexpi-c01.* byte-identical. Held out, run once with the frozen rule: OPEN100 sheets 6-11 0.872 -> 0.898 (P 0.899 / R 0.897; 348 of 388 true segments, 387 predicted; sheets 6 0.729 -> 0.826, 7 0.900, 8 0.862, 9 0.976 unchanged, 10 0.847 -> 0.906, 11 0.927 -> 0.901; junctions 134 of 139, 148 predicted; instrument connections 0.827). Sheet 11 lost one: at (1909, 932) a signal line with an arrowhead lands on the pipe from above opposite a valve drop from below; it had been read as a marked crossing because of the arrowhead's ink, and is now a plain crossing, so the drop is no longer piped to the main line (the key has a tee there) | 1 per development set; holdout 1 (single run) **(single run)** | **kept** | Holdout passes the pre-registered 0.88 with the frozen rule, but most of its gain came from the stray-pixel class, which was first seen on holdout images; the development gain on OPEN100 is small (+0.003), so treat the holdout lift as partly informed by the holdout, not a blind result. Known limit: two lines meeting a pipe from opposite sides with no dot (sheet 11) now read as a crossing | [EVALUATION.md: 7. DEVELOPMENT: everything already seen, now including the three cyclic sources scored once in A171 (UCI hydraulic 3 runs, MetroPT-3 4 runs, BattLeDIM 2018 14 runs) and all earlier row-6 sources (PRONTO days 2-4, SKAB 34, HAI 21.03 test1/test4, TE 21), which must not regress. HELD OUT, scored once with the frozen tool default: BattLeDIM 2019 (the evaluation year of the same Zenodo record 4017659, CC BY 4.0; 2019_SCADA_Pressures/Flows/Levels.csv staged into data/external/battledim-2019, MD5 checked against the record, SHA-256 pinned; never run through any band method before). Label mapping, fixed now, the same rules as the 2018 adapter: 33 pressures, 3 flows and 1 tank level joined row by row (5 min; ';' separator, decimal comma); normal CSV = 2019-01-01 00:00 up to the first leak that starts in 2019 (p523, 2019-01-15 23:00, exclusive), so the four leaks running on from 2018 (p257, p427, p810, p654) are part of normal; one run per leak in dataset_configuration.yaml starting in 2019 (19 leaks), score CSV = 2 days before its start to the earlier of its end and 7 days after its start, fault start = the leak start; leaks already running count as pre-fault rows. Criterion unchanged: detected by 3 flagged rows in a row at or after the labelled start AND at most 0.5% of pre-fault rows flagged. The tool's automatic choice decides the method on the held-out runs (no per-source flag); the drift method (A171) is scored beside it on the same runs for comparison *Hypothesis:* A method chosen and frozen on the three cyclic development sources, scored once on a year of data nobody tuned on, shows whether it carries over | Split and mapping only; no run on BattLeDIM 2019 yet. Known limit: the held-out source is the same network, sensors and demand pattern as the development source BattLeDIM 2018 (a new year with new leaks, not a new process family), so a pass shows carry-over across time on one network, not across kinds of cyclic process | 0 (split only) | **kept** | Development and held-out results are reported separately in the entries that follow | [GENERALIZATION.md](../docs/GENERALIZATION.md) |
| A183 | 2026-10-29 | Development (A178 split) of a band method for cyclic processes, csv_normal_bands.py. Variants: (a) operating mode detected from the data: the tag whose fit values split most cleanly into two clusters (2-means, separation >= 4 within-cluster sd, minority >= 2% of rows), each tag's residual from its per-mode mean, then the drift gap; chosen by held-out variance explained; (b) mode x time of day (48 bins); (c) time of day alone, 12, 24, 48 or 96 slots per day; (d) a median of 3 samples ahead of the fast average, with and without (c); (e) fast average 6 or 20 samples, baseline 288 or 1200 samples, on (c); (f) the median of 3 added to the existing drift method for every source *Hypothesis:* Removing a profile conditioned on cycle phase (the clock, or a mode found in the data) lets a step that the daily or duty cycle hides stand out, without flagging the cycle itself | Development, drift method (A171) before: UCI 0/3, MetroPT-3 1/4, BattLeDIM 2018 0/14. (a) mode: BattLeDIM picks pressure n726 (median held-out R2 0.49, more than time of day's 0.20) and passes 1/14; MetroPT's mode tags (TP2, H1, Motor_current) explain little of the other tags (median R2 0.01-0.15) and nothing is chosen; forced Motor_current x time of day (b, R2 0.36) MetroPT 0/4. (c) time of day: 12 slots R2 under the threshold (not chosen); 24 slots 7/14; 48 or 96 slots 8/14. (d) without the median: 24 slots 5/14 (with 7/14). (e) fast 20: 9/14 (24, 48 or 96 slots); fast 6: 6/14; baseline 288 or 1200: 7/14, unchanged. (f) drift + median on every source: UCI 0/3 -> 2/3, MetroPT 1/4 -> 1/4 (failure 1 lost, failure 4 gained), BattLeDIM 0/14; PRONTO, SKAB, HAI and TE pass counts unchanged, but every detection 1 sample later (TE fault 6 18 -> 21 min, SKAB other/8 1 -> 421 s). Non-cyclic sources: no time-of-day candidate (HAI's fit is 1.25 days, PRONTO and SKAB minutes, TE and UCI have no timestamps); MetroPT's time-of-day R2 is -0.003 | about 20 settings x 21 cyclic development runs; 2 full development passes (100 runs) | **rejected** | A mode found in a process tag can be moved by the fault itself (a leak shifts the zone pressure across the mode threshold, so the residual absorbs it: BattLeDIM 1/14); the clock cannot, so the phase kept is time of day. 48 slots kept over 24 (8 against 7 of 14); fast 20 (9/14) not kept: one run on one source, and it would be the only constant that differs from the drift method's. The median of 3 is kept inside the cyclic method (5 -> 7 of 14 at 24 slots) but not added to the drift default: it costs a sample of delay on every source for UCI's gain, which is a choice for the author, not a tuning step. Frozen as A184 before any held-out run (csv_normal_bands.py sha256 7713eceed011853f4e73764a577f90fd2202aac3d5e0d9408a367f7a76cd1463) | [GENERALIZATION.md](../docs/GENERALIZATION.md) |
| A184 | 2026-10-29 | Frozen on the A178 development runs (sha256 recorded in A183), then BattLeDIM 2019 scored once: csv_normal_bands.py --method cyclic, and a new default --method auto that runs it when the data is cyclic by the clock. cyclic: each value minus its tag's time-of-day profile (the fit rows' mean in each of 48 slots of 30 min; a slot with under 3 fit values uses the tag's mean), a median of 3 samples (a single-sample spike never reaches the averages), then the drift method's 10-vs-600-sample gap on that residual; band = gap fit mean +/- max(K x gap fit sd, 1% of the tag's fit mean), not scaled with the reading; K = smallest of 10, 12, 15, 20 with under 0.5% of held-out normal rows flagged. auto: cyclic when the first column is a timestamp, the fit rows span at least 2 days and the profile fitted on them explains at least 0.1 of the held-out rows' variation (median tag, each tag's level allowed to move); otherwise drift, unchanged. The threshold 0.1 sits between the two development sources long enough to test (MetroPT-3 -0.003, BattLeDIM 2018 0.20) *Hypothesis:* A daily profile taken from normal running removes the demand cycle that sets the drift band's width, so a leak's step shows, and the choice can be made from the data | DEVELOPMENT, drift (A171) -> auto: BattLeDIM 2018 0/14 -> 8/14 (auto picks cyclic, R2 0.20; detected 9, one run over 0.5% pre-fault: p810 5/576 rows; the 5 abrupt leaks p673, p538, p866, p183, p158 at 25 min to 2.5 days, incipient p257, p31, p654 at 1.6-3.3 days); UCI 0/3 -> 0/3 and MetroPT-3 1/4 -> 1/4 (auto keeps drift: UCI has no timestamps, MetroPT's duty cycle is not tied to the clock); PRONTO days 2-4, SKAB, HAI 21.03 and the TE runs: auto keeps drift on every one (fit under 2 days or no timestamps), results identical run for run. --method cyclic asked for explicitly: UCI 2/3 (flat profile; the median of 3 removes PS4's one-row blips; section 3's accumulator repair still flags 15/110), MetroPT-3 0/4 (DV_pressure pulses lasting 2+ samples before failures 2 and 3, 217-438 pre-fault rows). Level bands on BattLeDIM 2018 1/14. HELD OUT, BattLeDIM 2019 (A178 mapping), scored once with the frozen default: auto picks cyclic (R2 0.87 on the 15-day normal export) and passes 6/19 (p280, p710 abrupt; p653, p277, p586, p721 incipient), detects 13, 7 runs over 0.5% pre-fault (p514 10, p331 71, p193 129, p680 101, p426 63, p142 9, p123 4 of 576 rows; the tank level T1 among the flagged tags in 5 of the 7); 6 not detected (p523 and p827 abrupt, 4 incipient). Detections are late: 14 h to 5 days after the start (median about 2 days); the first flagged tag is the tank level T1 in 11 of 13. Same runs, drift 0/19 (detects none, no pre-fault flag), level 0/19 (detects 17, all 17 over 0.5% pre-fault) | 1 held-out pass over 19 runs (deterministic, scored once); 100 development runs per full pass **(single run)** | **kept** | Kept as an automatic choice: on daily-cycle data it passes runs the drift and level bands cannot (development 8/14, held out 6/19, both 0 with drift), and on every non-cyclic development source auto reproduces the drift method exactly. Limits: the held-out source is the same network as the development one (A178); 5 of the 6 held-out passes rest on flags 1 to 5 days after the start, mostly on the tank level, whose cycle follows the pump rather than the clock, and the same tag causes most pre-fault flags, so a detection here is a flag after the start, not evidence the method saw the leak. Duty cycles not tied to the clock (MetroPT-3) and data already averaged per cycle (UCI) are not detected as cyclic and stay at drift's 1/4 and 0/3. Not tuned further on 2019 | [README.md](../examples/historian-csv/README.md), [GENERALIZATION.md](../docs/GENERALIZATION.md) |

Final36CSV tests pass; independent reader52actualCLIs/261checks cover parsed UTC instants, physical spans, bad quality, row/text preservation and no-write/no-server refusal. Old/current valid reports and committed TE JSON/Markdown byte-match; detector functions/constants and replay implementation unchanged. N057 corrected from untried to already implemented under A163/A171; no new detector tuning. | Deterministic input-correctness controls and compatibility reruns on previously inspected sources; no new source family, held-out score or operational evidence | **kept** | Rejects an unsupported timeline before it becomes an engineering finding, while preserving originals, explicit preparation and the on-ramp/company-handoff boundary.
